This packet can also be copied manually. The following commands remain the manual fallback on the second PC; run them in PowerShell 7. The runner automatically enforces its minimum version. Extract the ZIP to a new folder on that PC, then adjust only `$packet` to its extracted `independent-destination-packet-1790998566441` folder.

In terminal A:

```powershell
$packet = "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop\Raya-second-pc-test\independent-destination-packet-1790998566441"
$root = "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop\Raya-recovery-1790998566441"
$hash = 'e44ed93611049e94bfb716af7a1ce6ad5c104f910d4244b67e823017195376fb'
& "$packet\raya-destination-preflight.ps1"
& "$packet\run-independent-destination.ps1" -Packet $packet -ManifestSHA $hash -Root $root -Stage Import
& "$packet\run-independent-destination.ps1" -Packet $packet -ManifestSHA $hash -Root $root -Stage Serve -Cycle 1
```

Import prompts privately for the separate archive password. Serve opens an owned server console and keeps terminal A supervising its process. Copy the printed loopback URL. In terminal B, set the same three variables and run:

```powershell
& "$packet\run-independent-destination.ps1" -Packet $packet -ManifestSHA $hash -Root $root -Stage Observe -Cycle 1 -URL 'http://127.0.0.1:REPLACE_WITH_PRINTED_PORT'
```

After Observe succeeds, press Ctrl+C only in the server console. Wait for terminal A to finish, then run in terminal B:

```powershell
& "$packet\run-independent-destination.ps1" -Packet $packet -ManifestSHA $hash -Root $root -Stage Closed -Cycle 1
```

Repeat Serve in terminal A with `-Cycle 2`, use its new printed URL for Observe in terminal B with `-Cycle 2 -Review`, close only the server console with Ctrl+C after success, and run Closed with `-Cycle 2`. Each observation includes a 65-second hold. Return the preflight JSON and all generated stage JSON receipts from `$root`; those results must be reviewed before destination recovery is accepted. If a stage refuses, keep its receipt and stop that cycle. Existing profiles and installations are preserved. Earlier progress entries below are historical.

## Install build prerequisites

1. Install [Git for Windows](https://git-scm.com/install/windows) and [VS Code](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/setup/windows). Sign into GitHub in your normal browser; Git may prompt you to authenticate when cloning this repository. Never paste a token into chat.
2. Install [Bun 1.4.2](https://bun.com/docs/installation), the version pinned by root `package.json`. In PowerShell:

   ```powershell
   iex "& {$(irm https://bun.com/install.ps1)} -Version 1.4.2"
   ```

   Open a fresh PowerShell window and verify `bun --version` prints `1.4.2`. If it is not on `PATH`, follow Bun's Windows PATH instructions in the linked guide.
3. For a Windows x64 VSIX with Raya's native process, desktop capture, input and accessibility helpers, install **Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools**, the **Desktop development with C++** workload and a Windows SDK from [Microsoft](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/overview/acquire-msvc?view=msvc-170). Current build scripts specifically expect `C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\VC\Auxiliary\Build\vcvars64.bat`. A different install layout needs a build-script change before packaging.
4. Install Node.js LTS with npm. The Windows FFmpeg packaging helper invokes `npm pack`; a bundled Node executable without npm does not satisfy this dependency. The new PC build passed with Node.js 24.19.0 and npm 11.17.0. Open a fresh shell and check `node --version` and `npm --version` before packaging.
5. Whole-repository typechecking also requires Java 21 for the JetBrains package. On native Windows, install Eclipse Temurin 21 JDK with `winget install --id EclipseAdoptium.Temurin.21.JDK --exact --source winget`, approve the Windows installer prompt, and open a fresh shell so its `JAVA_HOME` and `PATH` are available. Adoptium also supports an [archive installation](https://adoptium.net/installation/archives/) in a user folder with checksum verification and `JAVA_HOME`/`PATH` configuration. This PC now has verified Temurin `21.0.12.1+1` at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\EclipseTemurin\21.0.12.1-raya\jdk-21.0.12.1+1`; user environment variables are configured. The original MSI remains pending at its elevation prompt. The Raya Windows VSIX build has already passed independently of this Java prerequisite.

Build Tools is a separate Windows installation; it does not appear in VS Code's Extensions list. Raya's extension ID is `eden.raya`. Its panel lives in the right secondary sidebar; **Ctrl+Shift+P → Raya: Open in Tab** opens it as an editor tab.

## Clone and verify the exact source

In a fresh PowerShell window, choose an empty destination. This example recreates the familiar `Desktop\raya` folder under the new Windows user profile:

```powershell
$rayaRoot = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Desktop\raya'
git clone --branch main https://github.com/Kamil-Oseni/kilocode.git $rayaRoot
Set-Location -LiteralPath $rayaRoot
git rev-parse HEAD
git status --short
bun install --frozen-lockfile
```

Compare `git rev-parse HEAD` with the commit reported in the handoff chat. `git status --short` should be empty just after cloning. Bun's root postinstall prepares the native terminal dependency and local Git settings.

For an **existing** clone on the new PC, use this instead of cloning over it:

```powershell
Set-Location -LiteralPath (Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Desktop\raya')
git fetch origin
git switch main
git pull --ff-only origin main
git rev-parse HEAD
bun install --frozen-lockfile
```

If `git pull --ff-only` refuses because the destination has local edits or divergent commits, preserve them and resolve that Git state before updating. Do not reset the new PC checkout just to match the remote.

## Build and start Raya

For implementation work in a separate, persistent VS Code extension development profile, run this from the repository root:

```powershell
bun run extension:isolated -- $rayaRoot
```

It builds and launches VS Code with `.kilo-dev/` state in the checkout. `extension:isolated:clean` deletes that isolated development profile; do not use it when preserving its chats. To build an installable Windows x64 VSIX from the exact checkout instead:

```powershell
$env:RAYA_LOW_MEMORY = $null
bun run --cwd packages/kilo-vscode snapshot:build
```

The build prints its unique VSIX path under the Windows temporary `raya-vscode-snapshots` folder. After it succeeds, install **that exact printed file** in VS Code using `code --install-extension '<full path to printed .vsix>' --force`, or use VS Code's *Extensions: Install from VSIX* command. Confirm the running extension reports the same source commit before testing. The VSIX and local dependencies are generated on this PC and are not Git files.

If Bun reports `UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE` while Windows trusts the certificate chain, set `$env:NODE_USE_SYSTEM_CA = '1'` in the build shell and retry. This uses trusted system certificates and keeps certificate verification enabled. Do not disable TLS verification.

## New-PC memory budget

The October 3 read-only capacity check measures 31.38 GiB usable RAM, 18.74 GiB used and 12.64 GiB free. Ollama reports no loaded models; the RTX 5060 Ti reports 10,603 MiB free of 16,311 MiB. This is a desktop snapshot, not peak inference consumption. Keep the measured one-slot 32K local-model configuration while integration proceeds, and serialize heavy packaging with inference checks. The new PC uses the normal build path. Evidence: `.tmp/new-pc/capacity-local-20261003-integration-next.json`.

The October 1 inspection measured 31.4 GiB usable RAM, approximately 15.5–17.2 GiB free before inference, and an RTX 5060 Ti with 16,311 MiB VRAM (about 14.8 GiB free). These measurements replace the old PC's low-memory assumption. Use the normal build path above, and avoid running a full packaging build during inference acceptance.

The first conservative real-model gate used local Ollama with one Qwen3 8B Q4_K_M model (5.23 GB on disk), an 8,192-token context and one parallel inference slot. Auto's fixed instructions/tool schemas exceed that context before the first reply. The measured new-PC operating configuration uses the same weights with a 32,768-token context and one slot: the loaded model reports about 9.29 GiB of VRAM, with 5,346 MiB GPU memory and 15.47 GiB system RAM still free in the capacity probe. Both source and the matched compiled installation now pass three completed chat turns across two crash restarts, an actual metadata tool call, and one manual Routine reply recovered after restart. The private installed VS Code window also passes one visible real local reply and exact unsent Unicode draft recovery after Reload Window. These bounded passes do not establish broader Routine UI, scheduled workers or portable migration.

Model file size is not the runtime memory requirement: GPU weights, context cache, working buffers and the rest of the desktop also consume memory. Inspect Ollama's loaded-model receipt and RAM/VRAM headroom during actual inference before increasing the context or parallelism. The private `qwen3-raya-32k:latest` model configuration inherits the original Qwen3 weights and sets `num_ctx` to 32,768; the server retains its one-model/one-slot limit. Download success or a loaded context alone does not pass chat acceptance.
